method binding\"}\n  ],\n  \"text\": \"The GDScript virtual machine security boundaries in Godot 4.3 are defined across multiple architectural layers:\\n\\n**1. Bytecode Interpretation Layer:**\\nThe core VM interpreter is implemented in modules/gdscript/gdscript_vm.cpp which contains the main bytecode execution loop. The opcode dispatch mechanism is defined in modules/gdscript/gdscript_function.h with the Opcode enum containing over 100 different bytecode operations including OPCODE_CALL, OPCODE_CALL_METHOD_BIND, OPCODE_CALL_UTILITY, and OPCODE_CALL_SELF_BASE. The actual bytecode interpreter loop executes these opcodes in the GDScriptFunction::call method (modules/gdscript/gdscript_function.cpp:546), which manages the call frame state during script execution.\\n\\n**2. Function Call Frame Management:**\\nGDScriptFunction (modules/gdscript/gdscript_function.h) manages the execution state of individual script functions. The class defines the call frame structure, data type validation, and argument handling. This is critical for boundary control as it validates argument types and manages the execution context through the CallState structure, ensuring scripts cannot escape the frame context.\\n\\n**3. Object Method Dispatch:**\\nThe core method dispatch occurs through Object::callp and Object::callv (core/object/object.cpp:753, 735) which serve as the primary entry points for runtime method invocation. These methods resolve method names to actual implementations by consulting ClassDB and validating arguments. The security boundary here ensures only registered methods are callable.\\n\\n**4. ClassDB Registration System:**\\nClassDB (core/object/class_db.h and cpp) maintains the master registry of all exposed engine APIs. The ClassInfo structure contains method_map (HashMap of registered MethodBind pointers), which controls exactly which C++ methods are accessible from scripts. Only methods explicitly registered via ClassDB::bind_method are accessible, creating a strict allowlist. The api field in ClassInfo determines API level (API_CORE, API_EDITOR, etc.).\\n\\n**5. Method Binding Layer:**\\nMethodBind (core/object/method_bind.h) is the abstract base class for all method invocations. Implementations include validated_call() for type-checked calls and ptrcall() for optimized native calls. The hint_flags field controls method properties (METHOD_FLAG_CONST, METHOD_FLAG_STATIC, etc.). This layer enforces argument type checking and return value validation before native code execution.\\n\\n**6. Tool/Editor Script Detection:**\\nGDScript::is_tool() (modules/gdscript/gdscript.h:270) and the underlying tool flag track whether a script runs in tool/editor mode. The GDScriptParser detects @tool annotations at parse time (_is_tool in gdscript_parser.h:1329). EditorScript (editor/editor_script.h) provides a separate base class for editor-only scripts with explicit virtual _run() method rather than _ready() lifecycle.\\n\\n**7. Script Language Registration:**\\nThe ScriptLanguage base class (core/object/script_language.h) and its GDScriptLanguage implementation (register_types.cpp) register GDScript as a language. The is_tool() virtual method enforces that tool detection is per-script. This prevents scripts from bypassing editor restrictions at the language level.\\n\\n**8. Bytecode Code Generation:**\\nThe compilation pipeline (gdscript_compiler.cpp, gdscript_byte_codegen.cpp) generates the bytecode that the VM executes. This is a critical security boundary as unsafe bytecode generation could bypass VM protections. The compiler validates types and generates appropriate opcode variants (e.g., OPCODE_CALL_METHOD_BIND_VALIDATED vs OPCODE_CALL).\\n\\n**9. Callable System:**\\nCallable (core/variant/callable.h) and GDScriptLambdaCallable (gdscript_lambda_callable.h) provide user-facing method invocation. These wrap MethodBind and enforce the same security boundaries as direct method calls.\\n\\n**Key Security Boundaries:**\\n- Only ClassDB-registered methods are callable from scripts\\n- Type validation occurs at both compile time (GDScriptAnalyzer) and runtime (GDScriptFunction)\\n- Tool scripts are explicitly marked and can be restricted\\n- EditorScript provides isolated editor-only execution\\n- All method calls go through MethodBind which enforces calling conventions\\n- The opcode dispatch is exhaustive with no fallthrough to unimplemented operations\"\n}\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":9,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3174,"cache_read_input_tokens":59064,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3174,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":5,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5b7203e4-5a79-4379-93ac-e73e7a86b100","uuid":"e194c87d-26a3-4546-b697-d1eae21d42ce"}

These methods resolve method names to actual implementations by consulting ClassDB and validating arguments. The security boundary here ensures only registered methods are callable.\\n\\n**4. ClassDB Registration System:**\\nClassDB (core/object/class_db.h and cpp) maintains the master registry of all exposed engine APIs. The ClassInfo structure contains method_map (HashMap of registered MethodBind pointers), which controls exactly which C++ methods are accessible from scripts. Only methods explicitly registered via ClassDB::bind_method are accessible, creating a strict allowlist. The api field in ClassInfo determines API level (API_CORE, API_EDITOR, etc.).\\n\\n**5. Method Binding Layer:**\\nMethodBind (core/object/method_bind.h) is the abstract base class for all method invocations. Implementations include validated_call() for type-checked calls and ptrcall() for optimized native calls. The hint_flags field controls method properties (METHOD_FLAG_CONST, METHOD_FLAG_STATIC, etc.). This layer enforces argument type checking and return value validation before native code execution.\\n\\n**6. Tool/Editor Script Detection:**\\nGDScript::is_tool() (modules/gdscript/gdscript.h:270) and the underlying tool flag track whether a script runs in tool/editor mode. The GDScriptParser detects @tool annotations at parse time (_is_tool in gdscript_parser.h:1329). EditorScript (editor/editor_script.h) provides a separate base class for editor-only scripts with explicit virtual _run() method rather than _ready() lifecycle.\\n\\n**7. Script Language Registration:**\\nThe ScriptLanguage base class (core/object/script_language.h) and its GDScriptLanguage implementation (register_types.cpp) register GDScript as a language. The is_tool() virtual method enforces that tool detection is per-script. This prevents scripts from bypassing editor restrictions at the language level.\\n\\n**8. Bytecode Code Generation:**\\nThe compilation pipeline (gdscript_compiler.cpp, gdscript_byte_codegen.cpp) generates the bytecode that the VM executes. This is a critical security boundary as unsafe bytecode generation could bypass VM protections. The compiler validates types and generates appropriate opcode variants (e.g., OPCODE_CALL_METHOD_BIND_VALIDATED vs OPCODE_CALL).\\n\\n**9. Callable System:**\\nCallable (core/variant/callable.h) and GDScriptLambdaCallable (gdscript_lambda_callable.h) provide user-facing method invocation. These wrap MethodBind and enforce the same security boundaries as direct method calls.\\n\\n**Key Security Boundaries:**\\n- Only ClassDB-registered methods are callable from scripts\\n- Type validation occurs at both compile time (GDScriptAnalyzer) and runtime (GDScriptFunction)\\n- Tool scripts are explicitly marked and can be restricted\\n- EditorScript provides isolated editor-only execution\\n- All method calls go through MethodBind which enforces calling conventions\\n- The opcode dispatch is exhaustive with no fallthrough to unimplemented operations\"\n    68→}\n    69→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware.
